### Biography

**Atonu Roy Chowdhury** is a Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University. He completed his undergraduate studies at BRAC University, majoring in Mathematics with minors in Computer Science and Physics. His research interests span Number Theory, Algebraic Geometry, Additive Combinatorics, and Graph Theory.

Beyond academia, he is actively involved in mathematical Olympiad training. He has served as a trainer at various math Olympiad camps and was the leader and mentor of the Bangladesh team at the European Girls’ Mathematical Olympiad (EGMO) in 2024 and 2025.

In his free time, he enjoys reading, watching films, exploring music, and spending time with cats. He also has a deep appreciation for poetry and music, though he identifies himself as a failed poet and failed musician.

### Biography

Ms. Nusrat Zaman Raya is passionate academic and researcher driven by the belief that creativity and logic can coexist at their finest. She stood out for her academic brilliance, earning her placement on the Vice Chancellor’s and Dean’s Lists and receiving multiple merit scholarships throughout her school, college, and university years. Her excellence was nationally recognized when she was awarded by the Prime Minister of Bangladesh in 2017. Her enthusiasm for problem-solving was evident early on, as she became the Female Champion in her very first participation in BRAC University’s Intra-University Programming Contest. Her research interests lie in Data Science, Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning, specializing in NLP, Computer Vision**.** Currently, she is pursuing her Master’s degree in Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University, where she continues to deepen her research expertise and contribute to academic innovation.  
  
Outside the classroom, Ms. Raya is a vibrant soul with a deep love for the arts and photography. She continually chases the version of herself she has yet to meet, guided by the motto:**“Strength is built in the unseen battles.”**

## Dr. Md. Sakir Hossain

- **Source URL**: https://cse.bracu.ac.bd/faculty_profile/433/dr_md_sakir_hossain

### Profile Details

- **Name**: Dr. Md. Sakir Hossain

### Research Interest

* Network Security
* Wireless Networks (Mobile networks, WLAN, Sensor Networks)
* Biomedical Signal Processing (EEG, ECG, Medical image processing)
* Applied Machine Learning

### Biography

Dr. Md. Sakir Hossain is currently serving as an Associate Professor in the Department of Computer Science at BRAC University, Bangladesh. Prior to this, he held the same position in the Department of Computer Science at American International University-Bangladesh (AIUB).

Dr. Hossain is an accomplished academic and researcher with expertise in communications, cybersecurity, and signal processing. He earned his Ph.D. in the field of Wireless Networks from Saitama University, Japan, and received both his B.Sc. and M.Sc. degrees in Information and Communication Engineering from the University of Rajshahi, Bangladesh. He also conducted postdoctoral research at the Czech Technical University in Prague, Czech Republic.

His research interests cover a broad range of topics, including applied machine learning, cybersecurity, medical signal processing, and wireless networks.

Dr. Hossain is a Senior Member of the Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers (IEEE), USA.

### Research Interest

ML, LLM, NLP, Cryptography

### Biography

Ayon Roy is an Adjunct Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University, Dhaka. He graduated with a Bachelor of Science in Computer Science and Engineering from BRAC University in February 2025, graduating with the highest distinction in his program.

Before joining BRAC University as an Adjunct Lecturer, Ayon held several technical roles. He completed a Digitalist Internship in the AI ​​and Automation Department at Banglalink Digital Communications Limited and worked as an Digital Finance and Services Intern with the ML Team at NCC Bank PLC. During his studentship, he also worked as a Undergraduate Student Tutor/ Teaching Assistant in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, supporting core academic subjects.

Ayon's undergraduate thesis focused on the advancement of Bangla machine translation using informal datasets. This work involved creating a state-of-the-art dataset and applying sequence-to-sequence models to improve translation quality for low-resource and informal regional languages. His current research interests include Machine Learning, Natural Language Processing (NLP), LLM, Cryptography.

### Biography

**Soumya Swagata Biswas** is a full-time Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at **BRAC University**. He earned his Bachelor's degree in Computer Science and Engineering from Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (**BUET**) in March 2025. Prior to joining BRAC University, he served as a full-time Lecturer at United International University (**UIU**).

Mr. Biswas is deeply passionate about both software development and academic research. His undergraduate thesis focused on enhancing defense mechanisms in **Vertical Federated Learning** Systems through the incorporation of **Gradient Sparsification**. His current research interests include developing **adversarial attack strategies** for Horizontal Federated Learning Systems.

Beyond his academic and professional commitments, he is an enthusiastic learner with a keen interest in **astronomy**. In his leisure time, he enjoys watching **anime** and binge-watching **series**. He actively engages in research discussions and is known for his skills as an impromptu speaker. He is also deeply committed to **teaching** and inspiring others.

### Biography

**Sadif Ahmed is currently serving as a Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University. He earned his Bachelor of Science (BSc. Engg.) degree in Computer Science and Engineering from Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET) in 2025** He has served as a **Research Assistant** at BUET, where he worked on **Software Engineering Automation** and **Web UI automation**, contributing to projects that enhance the reliability and security of software systems through intelligent tooling.

During his undergraduate studies, Sadif also conducted impactful research on **secret breach detection** in source code and issue reports using **Large Language Models**. He has developed a wide range of practical tools and platforms in areas like blockchain, home automation, and anime data indexing.

His research interests span **Software Engineering and Security**, **Large Language Models**, and **Developer Privacy**. He is particularly passionate about using **Machine Learning** and **Natural Language Processing** techniques to advance software quality, reliability, and confidentiality.

Sadif is grateful for the academic mentors and collaborative opportunities that have supported his journey. Driven by a strong enthusiasm for both research and development, he aims to contribute meaningfully to the academic and software engineering communities. Outside of academics, he enjoys sports, gaming and watching anime and tv shows.

### Publication

1. **Secret Breach Prevention in Software Issue Reports**

* **Authors:** **Sadif Ahmed**, Md Nafiu Rahman, Zahin Wahab, Gias Uddin, Rifat Shahriyar
* **Venue:** Accepted at Technical Track of MSR'26 (Mining Software Repositories)
* **Summary:** Proposes a hybrid approach combining regex and language models (BERT/RoBERTa) for detecting sensitive data leaks (e.g., API keys, credentials) in issue tracker descriptions. Includes a curated benchmark dataset (25,000 issue reports) and a browser extension (SBMBot) for real-time prevention.  
  [Read Here](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23657)

· **Secret Breach Detection in Source Code with Large Language Models**

* **Authors:** Md Nafiu Rahman, **Sadif Ahmed**, Zahin Wahab, SM Sohan, Rifat Shahriyar
* **Venue:** Accepted at Technical Track of ESEM'25 (ACM / IEEE International Symposium on Empirical Software Engineering and Measurement 2025)
* **Summary:** Presents a hybrid method utilizing regex-based extraction followed by LLM-based classification to identify leaked secrets in source code. Demonstrates high performance using fine-tuned open-source LLMs like LLaMA‑3.1 and Mistral‑7B (F1 up to ~0.9852)  
  [Read Here](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.18784)  
  Check more at [Google Scholer Profile](https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=jLFo5SoAAAAJ&hl=en)

### Professional Activity

Department of Computer Science & Engineering

Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET)

(February 2025 – June 2025)

Department of Computer Science and Engineering

School of Data and Sciences,

### Research Interests

1. **Software Engineering & Security**

* Secret breach detection and prevention in:
  + **Issue tracker reports**
  + **Source code repositories**
* Automation of secure development workflows using machine learning and rule-based methods.
* Developer-centric security tools (e.g., browser extensions for leak prevention).

**Large Language Models (LLMs)**

* Application of **transformer-based models** (BERT, RoBERTa, LLaMA, Mistral) in software engineering tasks.
* Fine-tuning LLMs for:
  + Code classification and analysis
  + Context-aware security detection
* Research on hybrid techniques (regex + LLM) for high-accuracy predictions.

**Web UI Automation**

* Research assistantship involving automation of **UI workflows and testing**, using tools and scripts to simulate or validate user interactions.

**Machine Learning & NLP Applications**

* Use of **deep learning** in natural language and code analysis.
* Interest in **developer privacy**, **computational linguistics**, and **intelligent tooling** for programmers.

### Biography

**QURRATUL AYEN ELMA** is an Adjunct Lecturer in the School of Data and Sciences at BRAC University. She earned her Bachelor of Science in Computer Science and Engineering from BRAC University, graduating with Highest Distinction. Her academic tenure was marked by consistent excellence, earning her multiple placements on the Vice Chancellor’s List and the Dean’s List.

Ms. Elma’s current research interests are situated at the vital intersection of Computer Networks and Machine Learning (ML), with a broader focus on Artificial Intelligence. Her scholarly contributions include the development of "Risk Radar," a query-based threat identification system, as well as research into Brute-Force SSH attack detection and the application of NLP for online sexism detection. Complementing her academic work, she gained significant industry insight as an Intern at Aarong, where she worked on Generative AI systems for retail operations and predictive ML models for sales forecasting.

With a global academic perspective fostered through the OSUN Exchange Program at Bard College, New York, Ms. Elma brings a diverse, cross-cultural approach to her teaching and research. Before joining the faculty, she contributed to the university’s academic community as a Student Tutor for the School of Data and Sciences and as a FYAT Mentor, where she provided dedicated guidance and mentorship to first-year students.

  + Computational complexity and tractability
* Study of algorithmic techniques for solving computationally hard problems.

### 3. **Algorithmic** **Bioinformatics**

* Application of **graph-theoretic and algorithmic techniques** to problems in bioinformatics.
* Research involving:
  + Combinatorial algorithms for biological data
  + Graph-based modeling of biological systems
  + Optimization and enumeration problems in computational biology
* Development of theoretically grounded algorithms for biological and genomic data analysis.

## Farah Binta Haque

### Biography

**Farah Binta Haque** is currently serving as an Adjunct Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University, Bangladesh, since June 2025. Alongside her teaching role, she is also working as a Research Associate at Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET) from January 2025. Previously, she contributed as a Lecturer at Manarat International University (MIU). Her academic journey in teaching began as an Undergraduate Student Tutor at the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, BRAC University, from Fall 2022 to Fall 2023.

Farah completed her Bachelor of Science in Computer Science at BRAC University, graduating with *Highest Distinction*. Throughout her undergraduate studies, she was consistently recognized on the Vice Chancellor’s Honor List and the Dean’s Honor List for her academic excellence. She also earned performance-based scholarships in every available semester for maintaining outstanding CGPA. Beyond university, her academic achievements were distinguished by multiple merit-based government scholarships, including the **Talentpool Scholarships in PSC, JSC, and SSC**, and **HSC**.

Her research focuses on **Artificial Intelligence in Healthcare, Machine Learning, Deep Learning, Signal Processing, and Natural Language Processing**. She has published in both journals and conferences, including a paper on *Hybrid 3D CNN-LSTM for rs-fMRI-Based Parkinson’s Prediction* in the *Journal of Independent Studies and Research – Computing* and a conference paper on *Convex Hull Optimization for Mobile Finance Agent Allocation* at the *World Conference on Information Systems for Business Management*.

**Journal:**

1.**Eunus, S. I., Rokoni, S., Armisha, M. D., Omi, J. H., Islam, A., & Haque, F. B. (2025).** Hybrid 3D CNN‑LSTM Model for rs‑fMRI‑Based Parkinson’s Prediction. *Journal of Independent Studies and Research – Computing, 23*(1), 8–15. doi:10.31645/JISRC.25.23.1.2

## Masuk Ridwan Saumo

- 4th floor, Room No # 4P,
- Brac University,
- Kha 224 Bir Uttam Rafiqul Islam Avenue,
- Merul Badda, Dhaka, Bangladesh

### Research Interest

Data-centric Astronomy

Computational Astrophysics

Quantum Communication

### Biography

Masuk Ridwan is a EEE graduate with research interests spanning quantum communication, astrophysics, and cosmology. His undergraduate thesis was based on quantum error-correcting codes using Qiskit. Moreover, his research experience includes gravitational lensing and galaxy modeling in collaboration with NASA’s Space Telescope Science Institute, 21 cm signal observation and simulation with Center for Astrophysics, Space Science and Astronomy (CASSA), IUB and CubeSat development with Bangladesh Space Research and Remote Sensing Organization (SPARRSO).

His current research focuses on early-universe cosmology, including stochastic inflation, and modeling primordial black hole formation based on a Summer Internship organized by International Centre for Theoretical Physics (ICTP).

Beyond research, he has been actively engaged in community service and science outreach, contributing to educational initiatives for underprivileged children and organizing academic and cultural events that promote scientific awareness.

### Publication

* Adnan, S.M.R., Hasan, M.J., Al-Imtiaz, A., et al., Investigating the relation between the environment and internal structure of massive elliptical galaxies using strong lensing, A&A, 699, A259 (2025). <https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/202453239>

### Professional Activity

* Academic Commitee Member in the Bangladesh Physics Olympiad (BdPhO) from 2020.
* Worked in “Oxygen Support” during the COVID-19 pandemic organized by Ankur International.
* Volunteer in the Project Pothchola, initiated by Give Bangladesh from July 2022 to Feb 2023.

### Awards

* Awarded Quantum Excellence badge in Qiskit Global Summer School 2025 issued by IBM.
* Completed The EigenSolvers Quantum School (TEQS) Hackathon in the advanced tier.
* Bronze Diploma in Physics Cup TALTECH – 2019 held from December 2018 to May 2019.
* Silver Honour in International Astronomy & Astrophysics Competition (IAAC) – 2020.
* Honorable Mention in Mathematical Olympiad of International Day of Mathematics 2021 organized by Bangladesh Mathematical Society.

I have professional experience collaborating with frontier AI labs on training large models—covering RLHF, agentic workflows, and benchmarking.

## Faria Binta Awal

- **Source URL**: https://cse.bracu.ac.bd/faculty_profile/485/faria_binta_awal

### Profile Details

- **Name**: Faria Binta Awal
- **Designation**: Lecturer
- **Email**: faria.awal@bracu.ac.bd

### Office Location / Address

- CSE Department
- 4th floor, Room No # 4K84,
- Brac University,
- Kha 224 Bir Uttam Rafiqul Islam Avenue,
- Merul Badda, Dhaka, Bangladesh

### Research Interest

* Bioinformatics
* Computational Biology
* Computer Vision
* Machine Learning
* Spatial Transcriptomics

### Biography

Faria Binta Awal is a Lecturer (*On-Leave*) in the *Department of Computer Science and Engineering* at *BRAC University* and an Adjunct Lecturer at the *Department of Computer Science and Engineering*, *Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET)*. She earned her B.Sc. in Computer Science and Engineering from BUET in March 2025, where she graduated with distinction, ranking among the top 10 students in her cohort. She is currently pursuing her PhD in Computer Science at Virginia Tech.

Her research focuses on developing computational methods for life science, spatial biology, and biomedical data science. She has worked on projects spanning machine learning for spatial transcriptomics, graph-based biological modeling, and deep learning for biomedical imaging. In undergraduate thesis, Faria developed SuNSTAMP, a supervised factorization framework integrating spatial transcriptomics with complementary molecular readouts, and contributed to the Spatialomics-Toolkit, presented at the Festival of Genomics & Biodata Boston 2025 workshop.

She has received multiple academic and research awards, including the *RISE (Research and Innovation Centre for Science and Engineering) Research Grant* and national recognition in programming contests such as the National Girls’ Programming Contest (Champion, 2022). Her team (BUET\_Harmonica) ranked 23rd in ICPC Asia Dhaka Regional Contest 2024. Her ongoing work explores graph-based contrastive learning and computational models of cell–cell communication in regenerative biology.

### Publication

* **Supervised Factorization to Associate Spatial Transcriptomics with Complementary Molecular Readouts**  
  bioRxiv, 2025  
  ***Faria Binta Awal**, Robia G. Pautler, Md. Abul Hassan Samee, M. Saifur Rahman*  
  DOI: [bioRxiv-Preprint](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.09.28.679034v1.abstract)  
  Code Repository: [SuNSTAMP](https://github.com/f12-mou/SuNSTAMP)
* **Spatial Transcriptomics Data Analysis Workflow**  
  I worked with Dr. Md. Abul Hassan Samee on the literature review and the design of a pipeline that ultimately led to the development of the [Spatialomics-Toolkit](https://github.com/f12-mou/spatialomics-toolkit),  which was later utilized during live demonstrations at a [Festival of Genomics & Biodata Boston 2025 workshop](https://festivalofgenomics.com/boston/2025-agenda/mastering-spatial-data-analysis-basics-cutting-edge-innovations).

## Md Toky Tazwar

- **Source URL**: https://cse.bracu.ac.bd/faculty_profile/493/md_toky_tazwar

### Profile Details

- **Name**: Md Toky Tazwar
- **Designation**: Adjunct Lecturer
- **Email**: ext.toky.tazwar@bracu.ac.bd

### Office Location / Address

- CSE Department
- 4th floor, Room No # 4P,
- Brac University,
- Kha 224 Bir Uttam Rafiqul Islam Avenue,
- Merul Badda, Dhaka, Bangladesh

### Research Interest

CNFET-based circuit design, SRAM optimization, low-power VLSI design

### Biography

**Md. Toky Tazwar** is an Adjunct Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University, where he joined on August 4, 2025. He completed his Bachelor of Science in Electrical and Electronic Engineering from Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET), where he demonstrated strong academic excellence and a consistent commitment to leadership and co-curricular engagement.

His research interests lie in the field of VLSI design and nanoelectronics, with a particular focus on CNFET-based circuit design, SRAM optimization considering delay, power, and static noise margin (SNM), as well as low noise amplifier (LNA) design. He is experienced in industry-standard design and simulation tools such as Cadence Virtuoso, and is deeply interested in exploring efficient and scalable electronic systems through advanced device technologies.

At BRAC University, he is actively involved in teaching both theory and laboratory courses, including Digital Electronics and Pulse Techniques (CSE350), Circuits & Electronics Laboratory (CSE250L), Electronic Devices and Circuits Laboratory (CSE251L), Digital Electronics and Pulse Techniques Laboratory (CSE350L), and VLSI Design Laboratory (CSE460L). He is particularly dedicated to improving students’ learning experiences and has contributed to developing instructional materials for the CSE350 course.

Throughout his academic journey, he has demonstrated strong leadership and organizational skills. He served as the Vice President of the IEEE Electron Devices Society (EDS) BUET Student Branch Chapter, where he organized seminars, workshops, technical talks, and competitions such as the Three Minute Thesis (3MT). His leadership abilities were also evident from an early stage, having served as class captain during his school and college years, and later as a class representative (CR) at BUET.

He has received numerous accolades for his academic and extracurricular achievements, including being named to the Dean’s List at BUET (2021 & 2022). His earlier accomplishments include becoming a Regional Champion in the National High School Programming Contest, Divisional Champion in Srijonshil Medha Onweshon (Math and Computer category), and Best Student of Nilphamari District in National Education Week, among others. He has also excelled in national-level competitions such as the Bangladesh Chemistry Olympiad.

Beyond academics, he enjoys playing badminton and cricket, as well as traveling. As an individual, he is best described as analytical, leadership-oriented, and a dedicated problem solver, constantly striving to grow both intellectually and professionally.

- Md. Arif Hasan is a Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University. He earned both his Bachelor’s and Master’s degrees in Software Engineering from the University of Dhaka, where he developed a strong foundation in software systems, research methodology, and analytical problem-solving.

### Research Interest

[Google Scholar Profile](https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=U3sdpNYAAAAJ&hl=en)  
  
Software Engineering

Software Testing & Quality Assurance

Program Analysis

Human-Computer Interaction (HCI)

### Biography

**Md. Arif Hasan** is a Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University. He earned both his Bachelor’s and Master’s degrees in Software Engineering from the University of Dhaka, where he developed a strong foundation in software systems, research methodology, and analytical problem-solving.

Before joining BRAC University, he served as a Lecturer at Bangladesh University of Business and Technology (BUBT). He also gained industry experience as a Trainee Software Engineer at Cefalo Bangladesh Limited, where he worked on full-stack web applications and contributed to real-world software development projects involving scalable systems and collaborative platforms.

His research career began at the [Distributed Systems & Software Engineering (DSSE) Lab](https://dsse.iit.du.ac.bd/) at the University of Dhaka. His work focuses on software engineering, particularly software testing, Program Analysis, Software Quality Assurance & Human Computer Interaction.

He has also been involved in international research collaboration with researchers from George Mason University through a program affiliated with the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign. His ongoing work explores the intersection of software testing, program analysis, and distributed systems, with an emphasis on building tools and frameworks that enhance software quality and developer productivity.

His long-term research vision is to advance the field of software engineering by designing reliable, efficient, and deterministic systems that can operate robustly in increasingly complex and large-scale environments.

### Biography

Hi, this is Naveed. I am a Lecturer in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering with a background in Software Quality Assurance and Cybersecurity. I completed my B.Sc. from BRAC University and my Master of Engineering in Cyber Security from the University of Technology Sydney.

I have experience in teaching and conducting lab sessions at both undergraduate and postgraduate levels. Alongside academia, I have worked as an SQA Engineer, focusing on test automation, system validation, and software testing.

## Md. Alvi Islam Ratul

### Research Interest

Human-Computer Interaction, Human-AI Interaction, Responsible AI, ICTD, Usable Privacy and Security

### Biography

Ratul is a Research Assistant in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University, where he also earned his Bachelor of Science degree. His work sits at the intersection of Human-Computer Interaction (HCI), Human-AI Interaction, Responsible AI, and Usable Privacy and Security, with a focus on designing technologies that better reflect the needs of diverse user communities.

He is particularly interested in how individuals in the Global South engage with technologies primarily designed in the Global North, within contexts shaped by distinct socio-cultural norms, infrastructural constraints, and varying levels of technological literacy. His work critically examines how users interpret, appropriate, and sometimes circumvent these systems in their everyday lives, offering insights into the gaps between design assumptions and lived realities. Currently, his research focuses on the role of large language models (LLMs) across diverse user populations, with an emphasis on everyday practices, trust, and privacy implications. 

He has authored multiple publications in peer-reviewed international conferences and continues to explore pathways for developing AI technologies that better reflect and serve underrepresented communities.

In Proceedings of The ACM SIGCAS & SIGCHI Conference on Computing and Sustainable Societies (COMPASS 2026).

He developed his research career at Beijing Institute of Technology, serving as a Research Assistant in the School of Information and Electronics and later as a Graduate Research Assistant in the School of Computer Science and Technology. His research has focused on machine learning, deep learning, computer vision, and medical image analysis. He has contributed to projects involving *dental X-ray image processing*, *teeth segmentation and localization*, *bone age prediction*, *road damage detection*, and *large language model hallucination detection*.

In addition to his research activities, he has published scholarly works in cybersecurity, artificial intelligence, and image processing, including research on intrusion detection systems for IoT networks using machine learning and semi-supervised defect segmentation. He has received several prestigious awards and scholarships, including the Chinese Government Scholarship (CSC), Beijing Municipal Government Scholarship, and multiple international AI competition awards. His research interests include computer vision, medical AI, deep learning, natural language processing, cybersecurity, and intelligent healthcare systems.

## Dr. Badhan Das

- **Source URL**: https://cse.bracu.ac.bd/faculty_profile/525/dr_badhan_das

### Profile Details

- **Name**: Dr. Badhan Das

### Research Interest

My research lies at the intersection of **Computational Biology, Graph Theory, and Machine Learning**. I am highly interested in mentoring motivated undergraduate and graduate thesis students seeking to develop cutting-edge computational solutions to complex biological data. My lab currently focuses on leveraging deep learning and next-generation graph structures to decode genome regulation and population-scale genetic diversity.

#### **Project 1: Deep Learning for Cross-Species TF-DNA Binding Site Prediction**

Gene expression is tightly regulated by Transcription Factors (TFs) binding to specific DNA sequences, a process critical to understanding both human diseases and crop resilience. This research project focuses on developing advanced deep learning models (such as CNNs and Transformers) to predict TF-DNA binding sites in human and plant genomes. A core challenge we will tackle is **transfer learning**: training robust predictive models on the well-characterized *Arabidopsis* genome and adapting them to map regulatory mechanisms in the larger, more complex maize genome. Students joining this project will gain hands-on experience in cutting-edge genomic deep learning, cross-species model generalization, and plant regulatory networks.

#### **Project 2: Graph Neural Networks and Foundation Models on Pangenomes**

Traditional genomic pipelines rely heavily on a single, linear reference genome, which introduces a severe "reference bias" by omitting structural variants, insertions, and diverse haplotypes across a population. Pangenome graphs resolve this limitation by modeling an entire species' genetic diversity as an interconnected network. This project aims to explore the effectiveness of pangenome graphs across various machine learning solutions in computational biology, where linear reference genomes are typically used. Students will investigate the integration of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) and Genomic Large Language Models (LLMs) into graph topologies. We will tackle key challenges such as designing graph-aware tokenization techniques for genomic Transformers and adapting downstream prediction tasks, including variant calling and regulatory sequence analysis, directly onto native graph data structures. This project is ideal for students eager to work on advanced graph structures, machine learning optimization, and next-generation bioinformatics.

### Biography

Badhan Das is an Assistant Professor in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering at BRAC University and a former Postdoctoral Associate in the Department of Biology at New York University's Center for Genomics and Systems Biology. She earned her Ph.D. and M.S. in Computer Science from Virginia Tech (USA), following a B.Sc. in Computer Science from the Bangladesh University of Engineering and Technology (BUET). With an extensive background in both university instruction and interdisciplinary research, Badhan’s expertise spans Computational Biology, Graph Theory, and Machine Learning applications in Genomics. Her peer-reviewed work includes innovative contributions to viral evolution modeling, SARS-CoV-2 network dynamics, structural protein classification, and metagenomic surveillance tools.

At the intersection of machine learning, graph theory, and advanced genomics, Badhan’s current research program focuses on decoding the evolutionary and regulatory complexities of plant and human genomes. Her lab actively investigates deep learning frameworks—including CNNs, Transformers, and Graph Neural Networks (GNNs)—for TF-DNA binding site prediction. A key area of exploration within her group is applying transfer learning to translate regulatory models from well-characterized plant models, such as *Arabidopsis,* to complex agricultural crops like maize. Additionally, her research deeply explores the effectiveness of pangenome graphs as an alternative to traditional linear reference genomes, applying Genomic Large Language Models (LLMs) and graph-aware tokenization to capture structural variations and population-scale genetic diversity.

## Mollah Md Saif

- CSE Department

- Merul Badda, Dhaka, Bangladesh

### Research Interest

* Autonomous navigation and motion planning in unstructured or crowded environments
* GPS-denied localization, SLAM, and mapping using LiDAR and camera data
* Multi-sensor fusion and machine perception, including terrain understanding from depth and LiDAR
* Imitation learning and reinforcement learning for driving in dense, unlane-disciplined traffic
* Computer Vision, Object detection, tracking, segmentation, and scene graph generation
* Dataset construction and annotation for local road environments
* Multi-agent and fog robotics systems
* Embedded and on-device deep learning for resource-constrained robotic platforms

### Biography

**Mollah Md Saif** is a **Research Assistant in Robotics** at the **Department of Computer Science and Engineering, BRAC University**, an appointment he has held since **September 2025**. He received his **BSc in Computer Science** from BRAC University in 2024, where he was placed on the **Vice Chancellor's List** for academic excellence in seven semesters and on the **Dean's List** in one.

His research addresses **Autonomous Navigation and Machine Perception in Unstructured Environments**, with particular attention to road settings where lane markings and traffic signals are absent or are not observed. His work on **Autonomous Navigation in Crowded Spaces through Multi-sensory Data Fusion** was presented at the **2025 IEEE International Conference on Robotics and Automation (ICRA)** in Atlanta, United States. His current project, led by **Dr. Md. Khalilur Rhaman**, concerns **GPS-denied Localization and Terrain-aware Path Planning for Autonomous Ground Robots**, together with the construction of a large multi-sensor street dataset of Dhaka on which the methods are developed and evaluated. In parallel, he contributes to a research collaboration with the **Samsung R&D Institute Bangladesh** on mobile object tracking and scene graph generation for dynamic scene analysis, and he previously developed a job recommendation system based on retrieval augmented generation and vector search under the **BRAC Skills Development Programme**.

His professional experience spans embedded systems, applied machine learning, and space systems engineering. At *Sa.Ni.Corporate Srl* in Rome, Italy, he worked as an **Embedded Systems and Software engineer** on hardware and software for medical baropodometry devices, and at *Genofax* he served as an **Artificial Intelligence Intern**, applying deep learning to healthcare data and developing a bioinformatics pipeline for the taxonomic classification of bacteria. At the **Laboratory of Space System Engineering and Technology**, he designed an on-board image classification payload for a **2U CubeSat** and led the design of an **APRS payload** for the BIRDS-X open-source satellite bus.

He has been a member of **BRACU Mongol Tori**, the university rover team, since 2021, serving first as a control systems engineer, then as T**eam lead** for the 2022 to 2023 season, and as **Technical advisor** since July 2024. The team placed **16th at the University Rover Challenge in 2023**, the year he led it and operated the rover, and **8th in 2025**. At BRAC University he supervises **undergraduate thesis students** in robotics and perception, and he has taught supplementary programming classes at the BRAC University Computer Club.
